The informational\ntarget is *not* enforced — it is documented so trend analyses can see\nthe gap between \"still acceptable\" and \"design target met\".\n\n## Methodology\n\n* `N_PARALLEL = 15` — concurrency level matching the orchestrator\n  cap. Implemented via\n  `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=15)`.\n* `N_ITERATIONS = 30` — number of 15-parallel batches. Yields\n  `15 × 30 = 450` samples, well above the ≥30-sample floor the task\n  notes call out for statistical signal.\n* Each worker constructs a fresh `HomeIsolation` (frozen dataclass,\n  cheap), then times a single `setup(config=...)` call with\n  `time.perf_counter`. The dataclass construction is intentionally\n  outside the measured window — it is amortized across the eval body\n  the orchestrator runs once per eval.\n* After the timer stops the worker tears the HOME down with\n  `keep=False` so the scratch root does not balloon during the run.\n  Teardown is **not** counted toward the budget; NFR-PERF1 is a setup\n  budget.\n* Iteration index is folded into the eval id (`Perf{n:05d}`) so\n  workers across iterations never collide on the `mkdtemp` prefix and\n  the validate_eval_id regex (`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*(...)?$`) stays\n  satisfied.\n\n### Sample math\n\n* `p50` and `p95` are computed via\n  `statistics.quantiles(samples, n=100, method=\"inclusive\")[49]` and\n  `[94]` respectively. The same call is used in the NFR-PERF3 trend\n  pipeline so the two reports compare apples to apples.\n\n### Scope and non-scope\n\nIn scope:\n\n* Cost of `HomeIsolation.setup` end-to-end (`tempfile.mkdtemp` +\n  scratch-root materialization + FR-ISO2 `containment_guard` +\n  atomic-setup wrapper bookkeeping). This is the cost the\n  orchestrator pays per eval at the isolation seam.\n\nOut of scope for this baseline (tracked elsewhere):\n\n* Hook deploy cost (`deploy_hooks_to`). The adapter is **not**\n  invoked from inside `HomeIsolation.setup` in the current code; the\n  orchestrator (T03.16) calls it as a separate step. NFR-PERF3\n  (T03.21) covers the end-to-end suite cost including hook deploy.\n* PtyDriver spawn cost (COMP-007 / T02.16). Separate baseline if a\n  budget is ever attached.\n* Teardown cost. Implicit in the eval cleanup phase budget if one\n  is added.\n\n## Report schema\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"task_id\": \"T02.15\",\n  \"deliverable_id\": \"D-0035\",\n  \"nfr_id\": \"NFR-PERF1\",\n  \"schema_version\": 1,\n  \"n_parallel\": 15,\n  \"n_iterations\": 30,\n  \"n_samples\": 450,\n  \"budget_sec\": 2.0,\n  \"p50_sec\": <float>,\n  \"p95_sec\": <float>,\n  \"min_sec\": <float>,\n  \"max_sec\": <float>,\n  \"mean_sec\": <float>,\n  \"stdev_sec\": <float>,\n  \"overall_elapsed_sec\": <float>,\n  \"host\": {\n    \"platform\": \"<platform.platform()>\",\n    \"python\": \"<platform.python_version()>\",\n    \"machine\": \"<platform.machine()>\",\n    \"processor\": \"<platform.processor() or 'unknown'>\"\n  },\n  \"iteration_summary\": [\n    {\"iteration\": <int>, \"min_sec\": <float>,\n     \"median_sec\": <float>, \"max_sec\": <float>}\n  ],\n  \"durations_sec\": [<float>, ...]   // length == n_samples\n}\n```\n\n`schema_version` is pinned at `1` and bumped on any breaking change so\nNFR-PERF3 consumers (T03.21) can refuse to read an incompatible payload.\n\n## Report destination\n\nDefault path:\n\n```\nTASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T02.15/perf.json\n```\n\nThe destination can be overridden with the `T02_15_PERF_REPORT`\nenvironment variable. CI matrices that publish per-host baselines set\nthat variable so each host writes to its own well-known location.\n\n## Budget-failure handling\n\nWhen `p50 > BUDGET_SEC` the test invokes `pytest.xfail` with the\nrecorded p50 in the reason string. xfail (`strict=False`) is the\ndocumented \"host limitation\" branch the acceptance criteria permit so\na slow host does not red-light the suite — but the report still lands\non disk so trend tooling can flag the regression. The xfail path is\n*not* a permanent escape hatch: CI environments that keep hitting it\nmust update the host hardening plan rather than relax `BUDGET_SEC`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria mapping\n\n| AC (T02.15) | Where satisfied |\n|------------|-----------------|\n| File `tests/cli/eval/test_perf_home_setup.py` exists and produces a JSON report with p50, p95, and per-iteration durations. | `tests/cli/eval/test_perf_home_setup.py`; report schema above. |\n| p50 setup time ≤ 2.0s at 15-eval parallel (or xfail with documented host limitation). | Final assertion in the test; xfail branch documented above. |\n| Report `perf.json` is written to `TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T02.15/`. | `_default_report_path()` + override env var. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0035/spec.md` documents the budget and methodology. | This file. |\n\n## References\n\n* `tests/cli/eval/test_perf_home_setup.py` — the benchmark.\n* `src/superclaude/cli/eval/isolation.py` — the primitive under test.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/phase-2-tasklist.md` — task T02.15.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0032/spec.md` — COMP-006\n  integrated contract.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1431,"cache_read_input_tokens":137171,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1431,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":45,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9c1fec68-f25c-4dff-92bf-0af39ac90847","uuid":"4533a0bc-c22c-438b-bc28-c613c81ae16f"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01P5dQXBbz4bpNR82CQE38uq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01FnEiNyxFULLeksmygotHXu","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0035/notes.md","content":"# D-0035 — Notes (Task T02.15)\n\n## Design decisions\n\n### Why ThreadPoolExecutor, not multiprocessing\n\n`HomeIsolation.setup` is dominated by filesystem syscalls\n(`mkdtemp`, `mkdir`, `Path.resolve`); the GIL releases for every one\nof those. Threads give realistic parallel scheduling at a fraction\nof the process-spawn overhead and match the orchestrator's\nexpected execution model (T03.16 uses a thread pool, not a process\npool, because the eval body is dominated by I/O wait on the\n`claude` subprocess).\n\n### Why 30 iterations of 15 parallel, not 1 iteration of 450 parallel\n\nTwo reasons:\n\n1. The orchestrator caps concurrency at 15. Spawning 450 parallel\n   workers measures the wrong system — it would expose pool-scheduler\n   thrash that NFR-PERF1 explicitly excludes.\n2. Multiple short iterations let cold/warm cache effects average\n   out across measurements. The iteration_summary block in the\n   report exposes per-iteration medians so a future drift analysis\n   can spot a single-iteration outlier without re-running the\n   benchmark.\n\n### Why dataclass construction is outside the measured window\n\n`HomeIsolation` is a frozen dataclass whose `__post_init__` runs\n`validate_eval_id` (a single regex match) and sets one private\nslot via `object.__setattr__`. The orchestrator builds the\ninstance once per eval, outside the hot loop; timing it inside\nthe benchmark would inflate measurements with a cost the\nproduction caller does not pay per iteration.\n\n### Why teardown is excluded\n\nNFR-PERF1 is a *setup* budget. The orchestrator schedules teardown\non a separate worker pool (cleanup phase) and a slow teardown does\nnot block the next eval from starting. Including teardown in the\nsample would conflate two budgets the design treats as independent.\n\n### Why `pytest.xfail` is reachable\n\nThe acceptance criteria explicitly permit the test to xfail \"with\ndocumented host limitation\" if the budget cannot be met on the\nrunning host. `pytest.xfail` (not `pytest.skip`) is the right\nverb because:\n\n* It records the failure in the test report so trend tooling\n  notices.\n* It does not red-light the broader suite — a slow CI matrix entry\n  cannot block merge while the host hardening plan catches up.\n* It writes the report to disk *before* the xfail call so trend\n  tooling has the data to diagnose the host.\n\n## Observed numbers (initial measurement)\n\n| Metric | Value |\n|--------|-------|\n| p50 (s) | 0.0014567 |\n| p95 (s) | 0.0034431 |\n| min (s) | 0.0002415 |\n| max (s) | 0.0061760 |\n| mean (s) | 0.0016372 |\n| stdev (s) | 0.0009789 |\n| overall_elapsed (s) | 0.21751 |\n| n_samples | 450 |\n| Host | Linux-6.8.0-111-generic-x86_64-with-glibc2.39 / Python 3.12.12 |\n\n`p50 = 1.5 ms` vs the NFR-PERF1 budget of `2.0 s` — three orders of\nmagnitude of headroom. The headroom is consistent with the scope\nnote in the spec: `HomeIsolation.setup` does not currently include\nthe hook adapter call (`deploy_hooks_to` is a separate orchestrator\nstep). When the orchestrator wires the adapter into the per-eval\nflow, NFR-PERF3 (T03.21) will measure the combined cost; this\nbaseline anchors the *isolation primitive* cost in isolation so\nregressions in either layer can be attributed cleanly.\n\n## Open questions / future work\n\n* **Hook adapter inclusion.** If a future refactor pulls\n  `deploy_hooks_to` into `HomeIsolation.setup` itself, NFR-PERF1's\n  scope shifts and this baseline must be re-measured. The\n  schema_version field exists to flag that boundary if the\n  schema also changes.\n* **NFR-PERF3 linkage.** Suite-runtime tracking (T03.21) consumes\n  `perf.json` as one input. The exact diff predicate (regression\n  threshold, etc.) is owned by T03.21 and not pinned here.\n* **CI matrix integration.** Per-host baselines belong under\n  `TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T02.15/<host>/perf.json` — the\n  `T02_15_PERF_REPORT` override env var supports that layout\n  without a code change.\n\n## Anti-patterns avoided\n\n* **Measuring with `pytest-benchmark`.** The fixture-based\n  benchmark plugin's calibration runs would re-execute the\n  benchmark dozens of times; the harness wants a single\n  reproducible run with a documented sample count.\n* **`statistics.quantiles(..., method='exclusive')`.** The\n  exclusive method drops the min/max from the boundary calculation\n  which would give different numbers from the NFR-PERF3\n  consumer. Pinned to `inclusive` to keep them aligned.\n* **Re-using a single HomeIsolation across iterations.**\n  `setup()` raises `RuntimeError` on second invocation (the\n  \"idempotency rule\"), so the per-iteration construct is required.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2892,"cache_read_input_tokens":138602,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2892,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":38,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9c1fec68-f25c-4dff-92bf-0af39ac90847","uuid":"e004a796-e57b-4dc3-beb1-e993ed7a5e5b"}
